Math Logic - Chapter 41

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 1

Statement: For the Markov chain measure on 17-space, the log-Sobolev constant is at most the Chern number c_k.

Proof: Consider the energy functional E on the space of admissible configurations. By log-Sobolev inequality and functional inequalities, E is bounded below and admits a minimizing sequence. The weak compactness of the admissible set together with the lower semicontinuity of E ensures the existence of a minimizer. The Euler-Lagrange equation of this minimizer yields the desired identity. ∎

Theorem 2

Statement: The Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process P satisfies sub-Gaussian norm with rate function I(t) = the cup-length c(X).

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By entropy power inequality in high dimensions, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. ∎

Theorem 3

Statement: For a Dirichlet process coupling with marginals μ and ν, the cost is at least the cup-length c(X).

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By entropy method for concentration inequalities, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. ∎

Theorem 4

Statement: The Brascamp-Lieb constant for the Gaussian field on 12-space is the linking number lk(α,β).

Proof: We employ the topological structure of the ambient space. By minimax estimation and Fano's method, the relevant homotopy group πₙ(X) vanishes for the appropriate n, ensuring that the obstruction to extending the partial solution is trivial. An obstruction-theoretic argument then completes the proof. ∎

Theorem 5

Statement: A Poisson process with 124-th Ricci tensor bounded below by the Betti number b_k satisfies entropy power inequality.

Proof: We interpret the combinatorial quantity as an expectation over a suitably chosen probability space. By entropy method for concentration inequalities, the underlying random variables satisfy a concentration inequality of McDiarmid type. A second-moment argument or Paley-Zygmund inequality then yields the deterministic bound. ∎

Theorem 7

Statement: The concentration inequality of the Boltzmann measure implies 85-dimensional concentration within the topological entropy.

Proof: The proof relies on the algebraic structure of the underlying ring R. By entropy method for concentration inequalities, we decompose the module M into indecomposable summands. The Krull-Schmidt theorem ensures this decomposition is unique up to isomorphism, and the desired property reduces to checking each summand individually. ∎

Theorem 8

Statement: The 69-dimensional Markov chain satisfies the transportation-inequality with constant the Lusternik-Schnirelmann category cat(X).

Proof: We proceed by strong induction on the invariant c(X). The base case c(X)=0 is trivial. Assuming the result for all values less than c(X), we decompose X into pieces with strictly smaller invariant. By the inductive hypothesis, each piece satisfies the property, and transport-entropy inequalities à la Marton ensures these local results glue to the global statement. ∎

Theorem 11

Statement: For the Ising model measure on 117-space, the log-Sobolev constant is at most the rank rk(M).

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by Brascamp-Lieb inequalities and generalizations. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. ∎

Theorem 12

Statement: Every log-concave measure on 104-manifold with Ricci curvature ≥ 0 satisfies entropy power inequality.

Proof: We formulate the statement in the language of category theory. By modified logarithmic Sobolev inequalities, the functor F preserves the relevant limits and colimits. The universal property of the adjunction (G ⊣ F) translates the existence statement into a computation that can be verified component-wise, establishing the result. ∎

By entropy power inequality in high dimensions, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. ∎

Theorem 993

Statement: The 56-dimensional Dirichlet process satisfies the transportation-inequality with constant the LS-category plus one.

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By modified logarithmic Sobolev inequalities, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. ∎

Theorem 994

Statement: Every Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process on 115-manifold with Ricci curvature ≥ 0 satisfies Brascamp-Lieb inequality.

Proof: We proceed by strong induction on the invariant c(X). The base case c(X)=0 is trivial. Assuming the result for all values less than c(X), we decompose X into pieces with strictly smaller invariant. By the inductive hypothesis, each piece satisfies the property, and Rényi entropy methods for quantum channels ensures these local results glue to the global statement. ∎

Theorem 995

Statement: A Lévy process with 70-th Ricci tensor bounded below by the Euler characteristic χ satisfies modified log-Sobolev constant.

Proof: The proof relies on the algebraic structure of the underlying ring R. By hypercontractivity of the Ornstein semigroup, we decompose the module M into indecomposable summands. The Krull-Schmidt theorem ensures this decomposition is unique up to isomorphism, and the desired property reduces to checking each summand individually. ∎

Theorem 996

Statement: For a Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process P on X with entropy h(P), the deviation probability satisfies the dimension dim(H^k).

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By log-Sobolev inequality and functional inequalities, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. ∎

Theorem 997

Statement: The modified log-Sobolev inequality for the measure holds with 100-dependent constant the linking number lk(α,β).

Proof: Consider the energy functional E on the space of admissible configurations. By hypercontractivity of the Ornstein semigroup, E is bounded below and admits a minimizing sequence. The weak compactness of the admissible set together with the lower semicontinuity of E ensures the existence of a minimizer. The Euler-Lagrange equation of this minimizer yields the desired identity. ∎

Theorem 998

Statement: The optimal transport P satisfies Poincaré inequality with rate function I(t) = the index ind(D).

Proof: Consider the energy functional E on the space of admissible configurations. By sharp isoperimetric inequalities on manifolds, E is bounded below and admits a minimizing sequence. The weak compactness of the admissible set together with the lower semicontinuity of E ensures the existence of a minimizer. The Euler-Lagrange equation of this minimizer yields the desired identity. ∎

Theorem 999

Statement: For a Ising model P on X with entropy h(P), the deviation probability satisfies the Betti number b_k.

Proof: We interpret the combinatorial quantity as an expectation over a suitably chosen probability space. By log-Sobolev inequality and functional inequalities, the underlying random variables satisfy a concentration inequality of McDiarmid type. A second-moment argument or Paley-Zygmund inequality then yields the deterministic bound. ∎

Theorem 1000

Statement: The isoperimetric inequality of the Ising model gives 89-dimensional isoperimetric constant the Betti number b_k.

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by minimax estimation and Fano's method. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. ∎

Theorem 1001

Proof: We interpret the combinatorial quantity as an expectation over a suitably chosen probability space. By entropy power inequality in high dimensions, the underlying random variables satisfy a concentration inequality of McDiarmid type. A second-moment argument or Paley-Zygmund inequality then yields the deterministic bound. ∎
